=== ajay chauhan memorial fund ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/ajay-chauhan-memorial-fund/
THE FUND'S PURPOSE
A LIFE REMEMBERED
WALL OF REMEMBRANCE
WALL OF DONORS
CONTRIBUTE
CONTRIBUTE
IN LOVING MEMORY
AJAY CHAUHAN MEMORIAL FUND
CONTRIBUTE TO THE FUND
Goal Amount $200k (2Cr)
Ajay Chauhan (BT/CHE/1989) passed away on 8 September 2026. His passing has left his family, his batch and all who knew him deeply shaken. He is survived by his wife Reena; daughters Moha (IIT Kharagpur) and Mahi (IIT Guwahati); and his brother Alok, a decorated IAF fighter pilot (Retd.) who flew in the 1999 Kargil operations. A wife lost a devoted husband, two daughters lost a father who gave them everything, a younger brother lost the companion he had grown up beside, and his batch lost a very dear friend.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > ajay chauhan memorial fund]
Purpose of the Fund
Standing By Reena, Moha & Mahi
Ajay's batchmates, along with friends from his schools and professional circle, wish to stand by Reena, Moha and Mahi through this fund. The intent is simple: to see both daughters through their degrees without money being a concern, to support the household meanwhile, and to leave Reena some security thereafter. The ₹2 crore fund is intended to cover the family's education and living needs year by year, along with a modest reserve for contingencies and medical expenses. It also provides a corpus to support the family's future financial needs.
"Ajay would never have asked for help, so we are asking on his behalf."
Early Years
A Cantonment Childhood
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > ajay chauhan memorial fund]
Ajay was born in Jabalpur into an Army family and grew up in the cantonments of Dehradun, Pune, Chennai and Lucknow, moving as his father's postings changed. He finished his schooling in Lucknow before coming to IIT Kanpur. Sport found him early. During his school years in Chennai, he played Sub-Junior National tennis.
IIT Kanpur & Beyond
A Champion on the track, a Scientist for life
Ajay was a champion athlete who made IIT Kanpur shine in sports. He first represented the institute in lawn tennis at the Inter-IIT meets, then turned to the track in his final year, and 1988 became the year that belonged to him. At the Inter-IIT at Madras he took five gold medals and a silver, setting new meet records in the relays.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > ajay chauhan memorial fund]
After graduating from IIT Kanpur he joined Reliance at Patalganga. A few years in he gave up the job to spend the rest of his life on research, a decision that says more about him than anything else. His work ranged across agrochemicals, electronics, naval design, AESA radars and robotics. A chemical engineer by training, he worked just as comfortably in physics, computing and electronics design, and a good number of people around him did well commercially out of ideas he helped them shape, usually without being asked for anything in return. For years he also coached children preparing for the IIT entrance examination, with real dedication and entirely free of charge. He was a frugal, minimalist, and generous man who devoted his life selflessly to science, perhaps above all else.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > ajay chauhan memorial fund]

Tax Benefits: 100% tax benefits under Section 80G (India) for the old tax regime and Section 501(c)(3) in the USA via the IITK Foundation. Tax benefits in the UK, Canada, Germany, Belgium, and Australia may also apply as per local regulations.
How funds will be used: Contributions go directly toward tuition and living costs for Moha and Mahi, household support for Reena, and a reserve for contingencies and medical costs.

Stories of Change
A Timely Gift, A Stronger Future
Coming from a small farming family in rural Uttar Pradesh, Anshika knew early what financial uncertainty felt like. While a tuition fee waiver helped her enter IIT Kanpur, daily expenses like mess bills, electricity charges, and a failing laptop became overwhelming. At a critical moment, alumni support arrived. Pending dues were cleared, her laptop was repaired, and for the first time in months, Anshika could focus fully on her academics and placement preparation. This timely support restored not just stability, but confidence. Inspired by alumni generosity, she now dreams of giving back just as she was supported.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > annualgiving]
Anshika Singh
B.Tech, IITK Student
From Campus to Global Champions
When Team Aerial Robotics qualified for the global finals at ICUAS 2025 in the United States, travel costs stood between them and the world stage. Alumni stepped in. With support from the IITK Alumni Association (Outer Delhi Chapter), the team represented IIT Kanpur internationally and returned as global winners. Competing against top teams from across continents, their first-place finish showcased what becomes possible when alumni invest in student ambition. This victory is a powerful reminder: alumni support doesn't just fund travel, it fuels global excellence and institutional pride.
Team Aerial Robotics
Winners ICUAS 2025
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > annualgiving]
Empowering Ability Through Giving
Gaurav Swarnkar's journey in para-athletics is defined by determination and strengthened by support. With financial assistance from the Cell for Differently Abled Persons (CDAP), Gaurav accessed training and competition opportunities that transformed effort into achievement. His medals at state and national para-athletics championships stand as proof of what targeted support can unlock. Alumni and institutional backing helped convert talent into podium finishes, reinforcing a powerful truth: when barriers are removed, potential rises. Gaurav's success reflects the lasting impact of inclusive and purpose-driven giving.
Gaurav Swarnkar
Winner Para Athletics 2025
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > annualgiving]

=== campus school ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/campus-school
CAMPUS SCHOOL
Established on 14 July 1964 under the visionary leadership of Late Dr. P.K. Kelkar, the Campus School was conceptualized to provide quality primary education for the children of IIT Kanpur staff. With guidance from international education experts through the KIAP Programme, the school was built on a strong foundation of modern child development and teaching practices. It operates as a nonprofit under IIT Kanpur.
Academic & Co-Curricular Activities
This year, a variety of fun and meaningful activities were conducted to ensure every student participated. These included competitions like recitation, fancy dress, and story writing. Celebrations such as Sports Day, Teacher’s Day, and Children’s Day brought joy and excitement. Cultural events like special assemblies and science demonstrations enriched the learning experience. Students from classes KG to V went on excursions to places like the Kanpur Zoo, ISKCON Temple, and Bithoor. Community drives such as plantation activities and open house events further encouraged learning beyond the classroom.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > campus school]
Infrastructural Changes
This academic session brought numerous enhancements to the learning environment:
Swings in play area
New teaching aids for KG students
Refurbished labs, staff rooms, and office spaces
Installation of interactive panels, sound systems, laptops, and printers
New furniture in English, Math, and EVS labs
Collection of used electronic devices from IIT departments through “Our Community–Our School” campaign
Refurbished labs, staff rooms, and office spaces
Introduction of computers, audiovisual aids, printers, and sound systems
New wooden cupboards, sunmica tables, and age-appropriate furniture
PVC wall and ceiling panels with electrical upgrades for safety and aesthetics
Societal Impact
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > campus school]
The infrastructural improvements weren’t just physical upgrades, they were steps toward educational empowerment: Interactive panels made classes engaging and informative, the EVS lab inspired curiosity through hands-on learning, and a strong sense of community was fostered through parental involvement and teacher collaboration.
In every celebration, every upgrade, and every smile, Campus School continues to echo its legacy of love, learning, and leadership. From tradition to technology, we are moving forward rooted in values, reaching for the stars.

=== chandrakanta kesavan centre for energy policy and climate solutions ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/chandrakanta-kesavan-centre-for-energy-policy-and-climate-solutions
CHANDRAKANTA KESAVAN CENTRE FOR ENERGY POLICY AND CLIMATE SOLUTIONS
Established through the generous support of Mr. Sudhakar Kesavan (BT/CHE/1976), who currently serves as Chairman of the Board of ABM Industries and former Executive Chairman and CEO of Inner-City Fund (ICF), who led the firm to become the world’s first carbon-neutral professional services company—the Center is grounded in both vision and action. Anchored in the Department of Sustainable Energy Engineering, it aims to make IIT Kanpur carbon neutral by 2027 while emerging as a national and global leader in energy and climate policy. With a strong focus on research, outreach, and collaboration with governments and civil society, the Center develops scalable, science-driven solutions to address climate challenges. It serves as a dynamic platform uniting technologists, academics, and policymakers to shape a cleaner, more equitable future for India and the world. The Center is steadily progressing toward becoming a leading voice in India’s clean energy and climate policy landscape. Continued institutional support, strategic partnerships, and a focus on actionable research will be key to its impact.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > chandrakanta kesavan centre for energy policy and climate so]

OUTREACH ACTIVITIES
1.
The prestigious Dr. Chandrakanta Kesavan Best Research Thesis Award was instituted by the Kesavan Center at IIT Kanpur to recognize the best-graduating full-time Master's student from any department who has completed his Master's thesis in energy sustainability and climate solutions. The award is open to students of all departments. This year, the award was given to three outstanding researchers: Siddharth, Gudelli Shivakumar, and C Navya Nirmala, in recognition of their thesis work in this field.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > chandrakanta kesavan centre for energy policy and climate so]
2.
Held on 05–06 November 2024 at the Outreach Auditorium, IIT Kanpur, the Schmidt Sciences Conference on “Energy Technologies for India’s Decarbonization” brought together leading national and international experts. This high-impact, two-day event focused on innovative technologies and strategic policy frameworks to accelerate India’s transition toward a sustainable, low-carbon future.
3.
On 31 August 2024, the fourth edition of NetZero Dialogue was held at ATREE, Bangalore, focusing on “Socio-ecological Considerations for Renewable Energy Projects.” This one-day workshop fostered meaningful dialogue among researchers, policymakers, and practitioners, addressing the critical balance between renewable energy expansion and socio-ecological sustainability.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > chandrakanta kesavan centre for energy policy and climate so]
4.
The Sustainable GeoCommunities Workshop, held on 24–25 June 2024 at IIT Kanpur’s Outreach Center in Noida, focused on community-driven sustainability and decentralized energy systems, highlighting geospatial tools and stakeholder engagement in rural and peri-urban settings.
COMMUNITY OUTREACH
CKC conducted many Life and Carbon Climate Change Awareness events on Saturdays and Sundays in October 2024. These events were attended by over 200 enthusiastic students from Sunshine Public School, Prayas, and Opportunity School.The events sparked thought-provoking discussions on climate change, its causes, and the impact of overconsumption, racism, and unchecked ambition.
As part of a Cleanliness Drive initiated by the Chandrakanta Kesavan Centre (CKC), over 150 motivated IIT Kanpur students collected up to 300 kg of plastic waste from areas such as Ganga Barrage and parks like Kargil Park. These drives were conducted almost every Sunday from August to November.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > chandrakanta kesavan centre for energy policy and climate so]

DRIVING IMPACT THROUGH CSR COLLABORATION
At IIT Kanpur, we believe higher education institutes carry a responsibility beyond academics and innovation. Guided by our ethos, CSR here is not just a statutory requirement, but a shared commitment, where academia and industry collaborate to address society’s pressing challenges.
We partner with corporations to translate their CSR vision into high-impact, measurable outcomes blending innovation, empathy, and responsibility. Our curated programs align with national development priorities and Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), leveraging faculty expertise, student ingenuity and expert outreach partners.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
Join us as collaborators, mentors, and visionaries to create a future driven by academic excellence and inclusive development.
WHY PARTNER WITH US?
Frontier Research Leadership – Pioneering work in AI, Quantum Technologies, Cybersecurity, UAVs, MedTech, Sustainability, and Semiconductors.
Proven Impact – Successful deployments including AI-based PM grievance portal, indigenous UAVs, and affordable MedTech innovations.
Strong Innovation Ecosystem – SIIC and sectoral hubs nurturing 210+ startups and IP-driven ventures.
Trusted Collaborator – Partnerships with government, global industry leaders, and major healthcare institutions.
Societal Relevance – Solutions for Education, Sustainability, Healthcare, Clean Energy, Water, Transport Safety, and more—directly benefitting communities.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
SDGS ALIGNED STRATEGIC IMPACT AREAS
Healthcare
Supporting health of the underserved communities through affordable technologies, preventive awareness, and accessible delivery.
Environment & Sustainability
Addressing ecological challenges to promote environmental stewardship and long-term sustainability.
Education, Skill & Development
Fostering inclusive learning and skill empowerment through digital platforms, vocational training, and teacher development.
Research & Innovation
Driving problem-oriented, socially relevant research to extend the power of science and technology to real-world challenges.
Community Welfare
Enhancing quality of life in rural and peri-urban areas through participatory community development.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
Infrastructure
Building robust infrastructure for hostels, laboratories, and sports facilities for the holistic development of students which enhances academic productivity.
THE INNOVATION GRID
The Innovation Grid brings together IIT Kanpur’s ongoing research projects, innovations, and expertise across multiple disciplines in one consolidated view. It helps industry, CSR partners, and stakeholders explore research areas, identify collaborations, and discover meaningful opportunities to support impactful research.

We’d love to hear from you — whether it’s a question, a collaboration idea, or you're ready to make a difference through giving, we’re here to support your journey.
FREQUENTLY ASKED QUESTIONS
Is there a limit on tax exemption for CSR contributions made to IIT Kanpur?
The institute is eligible to receive contributions under Section 80G of the Income Tax Act, which offers 100% tax exemption on eligible donations.
Can companies receive reports or updates on the impact of their CSR contributions?
Yes, IIT Kanpur provides periodic impact reports detailing how CSR contributions are utilized, including progress updates, beneficiary data, and measurable outcomes of the supported projects. Transparency and accountability are key principles of our CSR collaborations.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
Can corporates collaborate with IIT Kanpur beyond direct funding?
Absolutely. IIT Kanpur encourages strategic partnerships that go beyond monetary support including co-development of technologies, setting up student fellowships, infrastructure development, and innovation-driven CSR programs aligned with the company's thematic interests.
Will IIT Kanpur provide official documentation for CSR compliance and accounting?
Yes, IIT Kanpur issues official receipts and utilization certificates for all CSR contributions, along with detailed project reports as per CSR compliance requirements. These documents can be used for statutory filings and internal CSR reporting.
What are the thematic areas where IIT Kanpur accepts CSR support?
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
CSR contributions can support a wide range of areas including education, healthcare, sustainability, clean energy, skill development, rural upliftment, and innovation for social good. IIT Kanpur aligns CSR projects with the company's focus areas to ensure mutual value creation.
Does IIT Kanpur assist in developing customized CSR proposals?
Yes, IIT Kanpur collaborates closely with corporate partners to design proposals aligned with both the company's CSR policy and the institute's research expertise. This ensures high social impact.
Are CSR contributions monitored and evaluated for impact?
Yes, all CSR-funded projects at IIT Kanpur undergo periodic monitoring and evaluation to track progress, outcomes, and long-term impact. The institute shares these reports with corporate partners to maintain transparency and accountability.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr]
How can a company initiate a CSR partnership with IIT Kanpur?
To begin a CSR partnership or discuss potential project areas, please write to dora_desk@iitk.ac.in or vp_development@iitk.ac.in. The IIT Kanpur CSR team will assist you through each step from proposal development to project execution and reporting.

Guidelines For Drafting MoU And Agreement
CSR MoU/Agreement Drafting Guidelines
Apart from the templates of Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) MoU or Agreement displayed on the website of this office, we also accept other Corporate’s MoU or Agreements subject to review and clearance by the CSR team of DoRA office.
The following guidelines need to be followed while drafting a Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) MoU or Agreement:
The Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Memorandum of Understanding (MoU) or Agreement has to be drafted in a professional manner so as all the legal and binding terms are covered in it.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr form]
The document has to comprise the following clauses and the same need to be mentioned in well-defined manner:
Definitions and Interpretations
Scope of Work
Terms and Conditions (including validity and renewal etc.)
Billing/ Invoicing and Milestone based Payment
Confidentiality
Relationship
Intellectual Property /Use of logos
Force Majeure
Failure to Enforce
Assignment
Duration of CSR Project
Notices
Amendment and Variation
Severance
Entire Understanding
Arbitration and Governing Law
Annexure (s) – if any
The explanation in respect of some of the above is as appended below:
Intellectual Property Rights (IPR): It is an intangible asset plays a significant role in building an Institution. It is also an important tool in economic growth and corporate donor tries to acquire it with the intention to get an advantage of monopoly profit. The Institute should try to keep its rights intact as far as possible.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr form]
Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) - Continued: The corporate contributing as per the mandate CSR policy is also availing tax benefits against the contribution. Hence, both benefits i.e. tax exemption and IPR cannot be given to corporate donor.
Payment and Others: The release of payment must be clearly stated. In this regard, the usual norms are as follows:
There has to be a milestone based payment schedule.
Budget Schedule with amount and date of payment is to be included as an article, clause or annexure to the MoU/ Agreement.
All Cheques/ Demand Drafts are to be payable to Corporate Research Fund Account, IIT Kanpur or Registrar, IIT Kanpur.
The CSR Administrative Charge (i.e. 5% of the total cost of a CSR MoU/ agreement) must be mentioned in the document.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr form]
Duration: The MoU/ Agreement shall be effective from the date of its signing and shall remain in force for a period of years or months (as agreed upon).
Project Investigator: Name of the Project Investigator (PI) must be mentioned on the first page of the MoU or Agreement. He/ She must be held responsible for non-disclosure of the Confidential Information received from the corporate donor.
Authorised Signatory: As per the prevailing norms, the Dean (Resources and Alumni) is the authorized signatory to all Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) Memorandum of Understanding (MoUs) and Agreements etc. The prior approval of the Director is to be obtained through a note file.

The Vision
IIT Kanpur envisions becoming the first IIT where no eligible student is denied support due to financial constraints.
As the cost of quality higher education continues to rise, the Institute is committed to ensuring that every deserving student who earns a seat at IIT Kanpur is able to complete their education with dignity and continuity.

The Innovation Grid
A consolidated view of IIT Kanpur’s ongoing research projects, innovations, and expertise across 14 domains. Explore the areas below to discover impactful research and meaningful opportunities for industry and CSR collaboration.

Healthcare
The Healthcare category is dedicated to research and innovation that enhance medical accessibility, efficiency, and patient outcomes. With a focus on scalable and technology-driven solutions, this domain covers key areas such as non-invasive devices, diagnostic kits, MedTech solutions, portable equipment, assistive devices, and biotechnology. Together, these subcategories represent our commitment to developing affordable, reliable, and impactful healthcare technologies, especially for resource-limited and remote settings.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr innovation grid]
SDGs Aligned: 3,9
FEATURED PROJECTS IN HEALTHCARE
Projects Driving Impact
01
NeuroGuard: Smart Wearable for Early Detection of Brain Disorders
NeuroGuard is a smart, portable wearable device designed for early detection of brain disorders. It enables continuous, real-time monitoring of brain activity, allowing at-home use and significantly reducing hospital visits.
IMPACT
By replacing traditional multi-day tests with a quick, user-friendly solution, it enhances accessibility and speeds up neurological diagnosis.
02
Indigenous Clinical Stereo Electroencephalography Electrodes for Identifying Epileptic Zones
This research aims to design and fabricate cost-effective, high-quality indigenous stereo-electroencephalography (SEEG) electrodes for precise localization of epileptic foci in drug-resistant epilepsy patients. The project will enhance accessibility to advanced neuro diagnostic tools and reduce dependence on expensive imported electrodes.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr innovation grid]
Read more
IMPACT
These cost-effective, locally made electrodes enable precise, minimally invasive brain mapping, aiding in the diagnosis and surgical treatment of epilepsy, especially in resource-constrained healthcare settings.
03
Plasma Patch for Skin Therapy and Wound Healing
This research explores the development of a Plasma Patch, a wearable device that uses cold plasma technology to promote skin regeneration and accelerate wound healing.
IMPACT
It offers a non-invasive, pain-free therapy option, ideal for chronic wounds, burns, and skin infections, enhancing recovery through antimicrobial and tissue-repair properties.
04
Capitalizing on cognitive networks for informing seizure onset in epileptic patients
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr innovation grid]
This research focuses on leveraging cognitive brain networks to improve the identification of seizure onset zones in epileptic patients. It explores how functional connectivity and network dynamics can enhance the precision of seizure localization and support more effective surgical or therapeutic decisions.
Read more
IMPACT
The study can lead to more accurate and individualized treatment plans for epilepsy, especially in drug-resistant cases. It may also reduce the risk of unsuccessful surgeries by improving the precision of seizure zone identification.
05
Portable and safe low frequency ultrasonic based medical imaging tool
This project aims to develop a safe, portable, and cost-effective 3D medical imaging tool using low-frequency ultrasound and Full Waveform Inversion (FWI) algorithms.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr innovation grid]
IMPACT
Unlike CT or MRI, it poses no radiation risk and effectively images both soft tissue and bone, making it ideal for broader, faster, and safer diagnostics.
06
Motion planning of magnetically steerable catheters in human arteries
This research focuses on developing magnetically steerable soft catheters for minimally invasive surgeries and targeted drug delivery.
IMPACT
By using pre-magnetized, shape-morphing polymer composites, the catheter tip can be remotely guided through complex body pathways. A physics-based model and motion planning algorithms will enhance precision and reduce vessel wall contact.
07
Portable Oxygen Concentrator for Vehicle & Transports
Portable Oxygen Concentrator for Vehicle & Transport is a compact medical device designed to deliver continuous oxygen supply during travel.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > csr innovation grid]
IMPACT
It ensures respiratory support for patients in ambulances or personal vehicles, making oxygen therapy accessible on-the-go, especially in emergencies or remote areas with limited medical infrastructure.
08
Hybrid Assistive Device for Transfer Mobility and Rehabilitation
This study aims to develop a lightweight, portable hybrid assistive chair for elderly and mobility-impaired users. Designed for lifting, shifting, and walking support, it assists in transfers (bed-to-chair, chair-to-toilet, etc.).
IMPACT
The chair features adjustable parts, easy assembly, and converts into a walker, enhancing rehabilitation and caregiving efficiency.
View All Projects

ACADEMIC INITIATIVES
Indian Institute of Technology Kanpur today enjoys an enviable stature in the world of academia due to its path-breaking high quality research and faculty who continue to explore new research frontiers and make significant strides in their fields. One of the prime forces behind this success is the institute’s alumni and well-wishers who from time-to-time have turned their attention towards supporting the institute and contributing generously. A large number of Faculty Chairs, Research Fellowships have been set up primarily because of the huge support received.
Faculty Chairs
Faculty Chairs have been set up to recognise the outstanding achievements of the faculty who enjoy a reputation for excellence in their research areas thus facilitating in their research. They are usually conferred upon a faculty for a period of three years and may get renewed based on his/her performance and interest.

=== gangwal school of medical sciences and technology ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/gangwal-school-of-medical-sciences-and-technology
Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Yadupati Singhania Super Speciality Hospital
About the Initiative
Transforming Healthcare and MedTech at IIT Kanpur
The Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology at IIT Kanpur has made significant progress in the past year, marked by robust donor support, major infrastructure developments, strategic partnerships, and a vibrant calendar of academic and research activities. The school’s foundation and growth are anchored by the generosity of Mr. Rakesh Gangwal, who donated ₹100 crores to establish the medical school at IIT Kanpur. This initiative, formalized through an MoU in April 2022, has transformed the landscape of medical education and research on campus. The school is named in his honor, and the adjoining super-speciality hospital is supported by JK Cement and named after Late Mr. Yadupati Singhania. The Founder’s Circle includes eminent IIT Kanpur alumni and industrialists such as Mr. Muktesh Pant, Dr. Dev Joneja, Mr. Hemant Jalan, and Mr. Anil Bansal, each contributing to the school’s vision and endowments. Additional support comes from leaders like Dr. Deepak Narula (Co-founder), Mr. Vikram Tannan, and families such as the Sutwalas and Parikhs, as well as corporate donors like Opalescent IT Solutions Inc.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
An insightful meeting of GSMST conducted with UP Industrialists aiming at its Futuristic Roadmap
Meeting with UP Industrialists
IIT Kanpur hosted an evening dedicated to the introduction of the Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology, and the Yadupati Singhania Super Specialty Hospital to industry leaders and dignitaries from the state of Uttar Pradesh on 15th March 2024. This event aimed at presenting the futuristic initiatives of the School and highlighted IIT Kanpur's ambitious initiatives to redefine healthcare and MedTech innovation in India.
The event was graced by distinguished guests, including Mr. A.K. Saraogi, President (CA & CRO) of JKCement; Mr. Atul Kapoor, MD, Regency Health; Mr. Neeraj Gupta, Joint MD, MKU Pvt. Ltd.; Mr. Raj Kumar Lohia, Chairman, Lohia Group; Mr. Deepak Kothari, Chairman & MD, Kothari Products Ltd., and our esteemed alumnus Mr. Gopal Sutwala, among others. Prof. S. Ganesh, Director, IIT Kanpur, Prof. Sandeep Verma, PIC Gangwal School; and Mr. Kapil Kaul, CEO IIT Kanpur Development Foundation (IITK DF); shed light on the vision, objectives, and notable achievements of the Gangwal School, the event serving as a platform to enhance understanding and nurture the partnerships between IIT Kanpur and stakeholders in various industries.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
Bhoomi Poojan of Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology
Foundation Stone Ceremony
IIT Kanpur held Bhoomi Pujan for the construction of the Gangwal School of Medical Science and Technology and Yadupati Singhania Super Speciality Hospital in the month of September.
GSMST at IITK signed an MoU with the Government of Uttar Pradesh
GSMST at IITK signed an MoU with the Government of Uttar Pradesh. The MoU was signed by Prof. Sandeep Verma, Professor-In-Charge, Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology, and Shri Ranjan Kumar, IAS, Secretary, Health & Family Welfare, Government of Uttar Pradesh, in the presence of honourable Deputy Chief Minister Shri Brajesh Pathak; Minister of State Parliamentary Affairs, Medical Education, Medical and Health, Family Welfare, Mother and Child Welfare, Shri Mayankeshwar Sharan Singh and Principal Secretary, Shri Partha Sarthi Sensharma, Govt. of UP. Prof. Amitabha Bandyopadhyay and Prof. Nisheeth Srivastava also joined Prof. Sandeep Verma for the event from the Institute side.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
Infrastructure Milestones
Construction on the GSMST campus and the Yadupati Singhania Super Speciality Hospital has advanced steadily:
Key construction milestones were achieved throughout the year, such as the approval of the fly-ash mix design, and M/s AGS started mobilization at GSMST and the completion of multiple structural slabs and columns.
Internal Quality Management System audit
Block Work Started at P1- First floor
EVENTS AND NEWS
The Uttar Pradesh government announced ₹50 crores in funding, to be disbursed over five years, further strengthening the financial foundation for the hospital’s construction.
GSMST was featured in The Week Magazine (June 2024 edition).
GSMST hosted delegations from the Armed Forces Medical Services and Tata Communications, exploring collaborative opportunities in research and healthcare delivery.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
IIT Kanpur signed an MoU with the ICICI Foundation to build a Digital Health Stack that will help improve public healthcare using medical technology, AI-based diagnostics, and better point-of-care services.
GSMST hosted the AIRTF selection meeting for the Anusandhan National Research Foundation (ANRF) to create networks and collaborations in STI and cross-sectoral areas.
A joint online research colloquium was organized on the theme "Exploring Biological and Chemical Strategies to Combat Antimicrobial Resistance (AMR).
As part of the Jalan Distinguished Lecture Series in MedTech Innovation, Gangwal School organized a talk by Prof. Muthuraman Muthuraman, University of Augsburg, Germany.
GSMST organized an exciting talk on "Understanding Cancers!" by Dr. Shabnam Bashir, Senior Consultant Breast & Colorectal Cancer Surgeries, Kashmir, under the aegis of Joneja Colloquium Series in Clinical Medicine on August 1st at 4.30 PM. The talk was organized at the Mehta Family Center for Engineering in Medicine at IIT Kanpur.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
IIT Kanpur signed an MoU with IIM Lucknow to offer an academic Postgraduate Joint Degree Program on “Healthcare Management" as part of Gangwal Medical Sciences and Technology. The MoU was signed by Prof. Manindra Agrawal, Director IITK and Prof. Archana Shukla, Director, IIM Lucknow. This program will equip students of several disciplines, doctors & medical professionals, with a contemporary blend of business administration with technological healthcare knowledge, positioning them as effective leaders to address complexities of healthcare industry & management.
The Innovations in NeuroTech Workshop was organized on November 21, 2024, by Dr. Arjun Ramakrishnan under the aegis of the Center of Excellence in Neuroscience, Neurotechnology, and Mental Health. The workshop provided a valuable platform for interdisciplinary collaboration among experts in clinical neuroscience, computational neuroscience, and neuro-engineering.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
As part of the Pant Workshop Series on Medical Technology and Future Medicine, the Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology (GSMST) organized a conference featuring expert discussions on “Innovative Solutions for Metabolic Health: From Research to Reality.”
The International Research Symposium on “Advancements in Precision Healthcare Robotics and Assistive Technology” was held on November 22–23, 2024.
SPARC Project Symposium on “Point-of-Care Healthcare Devices” and the Pant Workshop Series on Medical Technology & Future Medicine were jointly held from November 25–27, 2024. The events featured in-depth discussions on emerging medical technologies, focusing on scalable, patient-centric innovations.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
SPARC Symposium on India–Australia Cooperation in Medical Education and Biomedical Research was held on November 29, 2024, at the Melbourne Global Centre, Connaught Place, New Delhi. The event fostered cross-national collaboration and exchange of ideas in medical training and research.
SMST Flagship Projects were prominently showcased during “IITK Samanvay: Empowering Industry” held on November 29, 2024.
Workshop on “Promotion of Equitable Access to Affordable Healthcare in Uttar Pradesh” was held on January 28, 2025.
In collaboration with the Armed Forces Medical Services, GSMST organized a 6-day capsule course for medical cadets from AFMC Pune.
Several dignitaries visited the GSMST site, including the Director of IIT Kanpur, Dr. Deepak Mohan Narula, Mr. Arvind Singh, HDFC CSR Head, Shri Awanish Awasthi, Shri Manas Mandal, Prof. Anna Ryan, and Mr. Rakesh Gangwal.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
The GSMST Project includes Construction of Gangwal School of Medical Sciences and Technology at IIT Kanpur, covers Package 1 and 2 and commenced on 03rd October 2023, with a revised completion date of 03rd June 2026. The project encompasses the SSH, Academic Buildings, Ancillary, and Engineering Yard. Since its inception, significant progress has been achieved in both foundational and superstructure elements, with execution maintaining a strong focus on quality, safety, and adherence to timelines.
In the SSH and Academic Building, most of the key construction activities have advanced considerably. The foundation work is 99% complete, with only minor finishing activities remaining. The superstructure stands at 94%, with the upper floors in the final stages and work above the terrace nearing completion. Blockwork has reached 87%, with mid-level floors progressing and lower floors almost complete. Plaster work has achieved 61%, mainly on the lower floors, with a planned ramp-up in resources. Internal waterproofing is 33% done,particularly in wet areas and shafts. Flooring work has commenced in the common areas and IPD, with an overall progress of 20%.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
On the services side, electrical conduiting (surface) has reached 88%, progressing alongside blockwork up to the terrace level. Chamber and manhole work on the grade slab is 70% complete, a critical part of utility setup. Cable tray installation stands at 87%, with material readiness ensuring continuity. Sprinkler piping installation has reached 75%, actively progressing in shafts and corridors, while PVC conduit work is 86% complete, mainly in service areas. PIR ducting, essential for HVAC systems, is at 42%, with AHU floor levels under execution. In the Ancillary and Engineering Yard, the foundation work has progressed to 64%, with structural excavation and PCC work ongoing. Columns up to the plinth beam bottom have achieved 25% completion, and backfilling of earth stands at 11%, with continuous work in progress.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > gangwal school of medical sciences and technology]
Overall, the GSMST Project is advancing steadily in accordance with the revised schedule. With early-stage civil activities nearing completion, the project focus is now shifting toward services, MEP installations, and interior works. Sustained momentum, effective coordination, and strong stakeholder collaboration are ensuring that the project remains on track for its target completion date of 03rd June 2026.

=== givingback alumni scholarship for girl students ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/alumni-scholarship-for-girl-students.php
IIT Kanpur’s Alumni Scholarship for Girl Students
96.1%
Rs. 96,12,558
Contributed	Rs. 1,00,00,000
Goal Amount
75 Contributors
Give Back Now
As alumnae of IIT Kanpur, we cherish the values, friendships, and opportunities that continue to shape our journeys. Today, we have the chance to pass that gift forward to the next generation of women scholars.
The Alumni Scholarship for Girl Students is IIT Kanpur's first community-driven scholarship initiative, uniting alumni and well-wishers to support deserving young women whose families face financial constraints but do not qualify for existing government fee-benefit schemes.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback alumni scholarship for girl students]
In its inaugural year FY 25-26, the initiative supported 20 girl students with scholarships of ₹50,000 each, creating a meaningful impact through collective giving. A total of ₹10 lakh was utilized to support these scholarship recipients.
Your contribution can help a deserving girl continue her IIT Kanpur journey with greater confidence and focus.
20 more girl students in FY 26-27 | ₹75,000 scholarship per student
The Alumni Scholarship for Girl Students aims to bridge this gap by:
Providing scholarships to meritorious girl students in B.Tech. and 2-year Master’s courses.
Supporting those who fall outside government support norms but still face genuine financial challenges.
Enabling young women to pursue academics without the burden of financial stress.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback alumni scholarship for girl students]
Promoting holistic growth and development alongside academic excellence.
Eligibility Criteria of Scholarship:
Female students enrolled in:
B.Tech. Program: Family annual income between ₹8 Lakh and ₹15 Lakh.
2-Year M.Sc. Program: Family annual income up to ₹9 Lakh.
B.Tech. Selection Criteria:
Open to all undergraduate students, including those registered for dual degree courses or programs.
1st-Year Students: Selection will be based on All India Rank (AIR).
2nd Year Onwards: Evaluated on the basis of highest CPI.
Retention: In case of retention, students must maintain a minimum CPI of 7.0.
M.Sc. Selection Criteria:
1st-Year Students: Candidates will be evaluated and shortlisted on the basis of their JAM score (highest score given priority).
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback alumni scholarship for girl students]
2nd-Year Students: Selection will be made on the basis of highest CPI, with preference given to previous beneficiaries who maintain a minimum CPI of 7.0.
General Preference: If a student is already receiving any form of institutional or government support, preference will be given to a student who is not availing any such scholarship.
Scholarship Distribution: To maximize impact, the allocation of the 20 total scholarships will remain flexible between the B.Tech and M.Sc. programs based on applicant merit in a given academic session.

=== givingback animal allies ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/animal-allies.php
 DoRA |
 Animal Allies Wing
4.7%
Rs. 51,087
Contributed	Rs. 10,83,000
Goal Amount
17 Contributors
Give Back Now
An owlet, too weak to fly, lay beneath the tree from which it had fallen. A few IIT Kanpur students stopped, gently cared for the injured bird, and nursed it back to health until it was ready to be released safely into its natural habitat.
This is not an isolated story.
Across the IIT Kanpur campus, acts of compassion unfold quietly every day; a roadside turtle carried to safety during the monsoon, a bull rescued and treated during the harsh winter months, injured birds provided timely care, and orphaned puppies nurtured until they found loving homes. Behind every rescue is a student who simply chose not to look away. Spread across more than 1200 acres, the IIT Kanpur campus is home to a rich diversity of fauna, birds, mammals, reptiles, and stray animals. Caring for this shared ecosystem reflects the Institute's commitment to compassion, environmental stewardship, and responsible community engagement. With a deep sense of giving back to our alma mater, IIT Kanpur is establishing the Animal Allies Wing under the Community Welfare Cell a structured, student-led initiative dedicated to strengthening and sustaining animal welfare on campus.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback animal allies]
While the Institute Animal Welfare Cell coordinates vaccination and sterilisation drives, much of the day-to-day work of rescue, emergency response, treatment, rehabilitation, and adoption continue to depend on dedicated student volunteers. Their commitment has saved many animal lives, but these efforts largely rely on informal networks and personal experience. We have handled 300+ cases since 2024, when formal documentation of these cases began.
As each graduating batch leaves, valuable knowledge and experience leave with them. New volunteers often learn through experience rather than structured training, and support for emergency veterinary care and rehabilitation remains limited. The compassion has always been present; what is needed now is a sustainable system that ensures this care continues for years to come.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback animal allies]
The Animal Allies Wing seeks to bridge this gap by:
Coordinating campus rescue operations in collaboration with trusted local animal welfare organisations.
Training and mentoring student volunteers in safe animal handling and emergency response, ensuring that knowledge is passed on from one generation of students to the next.
Supporting emergency veterinary treatment, medicines, vaccinations, rehabilitation, and essential rescue resources for injured and sick animals.
Organising awareness programmes and adoption drives to foster a more compassionate and responsible campus community.
Organising programmes and events related to biodiversity conservation within campus area, in consultation with experts in the field.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback animal allies]
As part of our welfare initiative, we intend to provide anti-rabies vaccination coverage to non-contractual campus workers, such as mess workers, sanitation workers, gardeners, and other similar staff, who are currently not eligible to receive these vaccinations free of cost through the Health Centre.
Your contribution will help establish a lasting support system for animal welfare at IIT Kanpur. Every donation will directly strengthen rescue efforts, volunteer training, emergency medical care, rehabilitation, and awareness initiatives, ensuring that timely help is available whenever an animal in distress is found on campus. By supporting the Animal Allies Wing, you are helping build a sustainable legacy of compassion, one that empowers future generations of students to continue caring for the animals that share our campus. Together, we can ensure that campus fauna receives the care, protection, and compassion it deserves. Support the Animal Allies Wing. Help us build a kinder IIT Kanpur one rescued life at a time. BUDGET – Rs. 10.83 Lakhs
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback animal allies]
Some snippets from our recent adoption drive

=== givingback awards ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/awards
Student Award / Medal / Prize
Give Back Now
A number of Annual Cash awards/ Gold Medal and Silver Medals have been created in the institute with the help of donations received from its alumni, former Faculty members, well-wishers and friends to encourage meritorious students/faculty and staff in the institute. We have around 75 Endowment awards which include student awards, faculty awards, staff awards, community awards & awards for school children.
The student awards are given to the student recipients during Convocation ceremony and during Academic Excellence award Ceremony. These awards instill motivation and enthusiasm in the students to excel in their academic endeavor and also provide financial assistance to meritorious and needy students.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback awards]
Few Cash awards have been created for Institute Faculty members from the donor's fund. These are awarded on Teacher's Day (in case of Institute award) and Department's Day (in case of departmental award)
A number of awards have been created from the donations for children studying in the schools located in IIT Kanpur campus. Similarly, institute has created many Community awards for faculty and staff members to encourage community services.

Options	Total Endowment required (in Rs)

Campus School, established in the year 1964, emphasizes on 'learning by doing'. The school has a well-balanced curriculum based on CBSE pattern. The management of the school believes in continuous evaluation of students' growth and development spread over the entire academic session. The diagnosis of learning gaps, use of corrective measures, and feedback to teachers and students is a continuous activity which helps achieve the overall objective and growth of the School.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback campus school]
The campus school not only caters to the children of faculty and staff, but also to the children of diverse sections of the IIT Kanpur community including gardeners, security staff, domestic helpers, and children from the nearby villages of the campus.

Growth at IIT Kanpur:
In recent years, IITK has seen exponential growth in the student population. The undergraduate count has grown from 1,200 back in 1965 to over 4,000 today, and post graduates have grown from a few hundred to over 3,000. This requires a huge growth in campus infrastructure from academic buildings, labs and lecture halls to hostel and staff accommodation.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class 1970]

The Batch collectively wishes to raise Rs.1.5 Crore to support funding for these capital projects which will elevate the existing infrastructure and ecosystem in the campus and in turn ensure better facilities for the students and the academia.

=== givingback class 1973 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-1973.php
 Class of 1973
Give Back Now
1973 Class Fund
In your Golden Jubilee year of graduation, we extend our heartfelt gratitude to your unrelenting dedication and selfless contributions, whether financial or otherwise, and the significant impact they have had in the growth and success of IIT Kanpur. You are all ambassadors of this great institution, and we are very proud of your accomplishments and are thrilled to see you reach new heights and touch the lives of so many people.
Giving back plays a vital role in supporting various initiatives and programs that directly benefit the IITK community in the form of:
Student Scholarships & Awards
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class 1973]
Faculty Chairs & Fellowships
Community Welfare
Infrastructure Upgrades
Research & Development Facilities
These initiatives and programs directly impact the institute by improving the student's Living and Learning experience, providing financial aid to underprivileged students, supporting Faculty Initiatives and strengthening the Research & Innovation Ecosystem at IITK. This year promises new beginnings and opportunities and have already generated 50+ Faculty Chairs, 15+ Faculty Fellowships, 70+ Student Awards and 150+ Student Scholarships.
Your generosity, no matter how large or small, to giving back to your alma mater, has always played an important part in fostering the Institute's culture of excellence. Your Class of 1973 has empowered our students by offering many financial support initiatives. Notable among these initiatives, the Class of 1973 Scholarships , have made a big difference to the recipients by encouraging creativity and providing great educational opportunities.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class 1973]
In your Golden Jubilee year of graduation, please continue your amazing support for all these initiatives. The increase in the corpus of Class of 1973 contributions, will go a long way in supporting the education of more students. We gratefully acknowledge and appreciate your selfless patronage from the bottom of our hearts.

=== givingback class 1979 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-1979
 Class of 1979
Give Back Now
IIT Kanpur Alumni of 1979 class contributed to set up Young Faculty Research Fellowship. The Batch has collectively contributed with an aim to promote and support teaching and research at IIT Kanpur. This valuable benefaction has been of immense help in instituting two faculty chairs-Pandit Girish Ranjan & Sushama Rani Pathak Chair, Pandit Ramchandra Dwivedi Chair, USV Chair and four scholarships-Pushpa Garg Scholarship, S. Y. Jakatdar Memorial Scholarship, Shri Ram Sahai Agarwal Scholarship, Smt. Vidyawati Agarwal Scholarship and Smt. Tara Ghate’s Scholarship and M.A. Pai Scholarship.

=== givingback class 1980 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-1980
 Class of 1980
Give Back Now
Over the last four decades, the Class of 1980 has contributed generously a sum of INR. 1.738 crores to the institute. This valuable benefaction has been of immense help to the institute in creating 69-80 Outreach building, 3 Student Scholarships, 3 student awards, 1 Faculty Award, providing travel grant to the students for attending International Conferences, Cash awards to students for publishing research papers in reputed journals, various research activities for Academic Departments, support towards various schools in the campus, Medical Emergency Fund, Alumni Memorial Fund, contract workers and mess workers welfare fund and supporting Students-Undergraduate Research Graduate Excellence (SURGE) program at IITKanpur.

 Class of 1981
Give Back Now

=== givingback class 1983 ===

Class of 1983 – 40th year reunion Legacy project @ IIT Kanpur
Give Back Now
Pledge Now
The Class of 1983 has always supported many initiatives at IIT Kanpur over the years. Keeping up with their philanthropic gesture, the institute is proud to announce the Class of 1983 batch fund supported for the construction of additional blocks in Hall of residence 3. This enhanced capacity will help students get better living standards at the campus and focus better on their education.
Join your batchmates in supporting your batch legacy project!
If you need any help or have any queries, kindly contact us. We wish to reiterate that your contribution, no matter how big or small is critical for sustaining excellence at your alma mater. We look forward to receiving your continued support and aided patronage as always.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class 1983]
If you need any help or have any queries, kindly contact us. We wish to reiterate that your contribution, no matter how big or small is critical for sustaining excellence at your alma mater. We look forward to receiving your continued support and aided patronage as always.
Tax Benefits
For India Contributions are 100% Tax deductible u/s 80G of income Tax Act, 1961.
For US Contribution are 100% Tax deductible u/s 501(c)(3) of US internal revenue code. You can also donate through your company's Matching Grant Program. Please look for Indian Institute of Technology Kanpur Foundation Inc. in your organisation's list of approved organisations.

=== givingback class 1991 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-1991
Class of 1991(Batch-1987) – 30th year reunion Legacy project @ IIT Kanpur
Give Back Now
Pledge Now
A Legacy Project is a project adopted by the batch celebrating its Golden Jubilee Reunion, for the betterment of the Institute and of the people associated with it. It is a way of honouring your alma mater and to create a lasting legacy in remembrance of your years at IIT Kanpur
How will this work?
The funds donated by the batch will be parked in the Batch legacy project for the Class of 1991
The legacy project for the Class of 1991 will be decide in due course
These projects and endowments will be based on the concensus of the batch
Periodic updates will be provided on the status of the funds utilised

Class of 1996 – Silver Jubilee Legacy Project
Give Back Now
Pledge Now
A Legacy Project is adopted by the batch celebrating its Silver Jubilee Reunion milestone. This project is to support the Institute in its endeavour in becoming one of the leading global institutes and also to support initiatives for the welfare of the people associated with it. Giving back is a way of honouring your alma mater and also creates a lasting legacy in remembrance of your cherished years at IIT Kanpur.
Join your batchmates in supporting Class of 1996 Silver Jubilee Legacy Project!
How will this work?
The funds can be donated over the next one year
The funds donated by the batch will be parked in the Batch legacy project for the Class of 1996

=== givingback class of 1974 legacy fund ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-of-1974-legacy-fund.php
Class of 1974 Legacy Fund
 No of Contributors 27
Give Back Now
 No of Pledges 82 Pledge Now
IIT Kanpur has experienced remarkable expansion in recent times, particularly in its student enrollment. Since 1965, the undergraduate population has surged from 1,200 to over 5000, while postgraduate numbers have grown from a few hundred to surpass 4000. This substantial increase necessitates significant development in campus infrastructure, encompassing academic buildings, laboratories, lecture halls, hostels and staff accommodation etc.
The Class of 1974 has initiated a campaign to contribute to IIT Kanpur's ongoing growth and its visionary plans for the future. Their objective is to raise funds to support the critical needs of the institute i.e. Infrastructure projects aimed at improving the living and learning experience of the students, strengthening the R&D ecosystem and lastly providing financial aid to the students from marginalised family backgrounds.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class of 1974 legacy fund]

=== givingback class of 1975 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-of-1975.php
IITK Class-of-75 Funding
Give Back Now Pledge Now
As the Class of 1975 gets excited about its Golden Jubilee reunion, the euphoria to meet classmates, the nostalgia of walking through the hallowed corridors, and the remembrance of exploring the campus on rickety bicycles is only building up, right? This reunion will provide the Class an opportunity to relive shared experiences and witness the massive growth and expansion of their alma mater since their graduation fifty years ago!
With a sense of nostalgia and love for their alma mater, the Class of 1975 has come forward to support the growth and vision pledging their support to modernize the campus and provide state-of-the-art facilities for students, faculty, and IITK community at large. By giving back to IITK, the Class of 1975 will ensure that future generations of the institute have access to world-class educational facilities. The Class of 1975 is also ensuring to leave a legacy, setting an example of those who contributed to the development of IIT Kanpur. Their generosity demonstrates their commitment to supporting and believing in the vision of the institute.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class of 1975]

=== givingback class of 1998 legacy project ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-of-1998-legacy-project.php
Class of 1998 Legacy Project
 No of Contributors 100
Give Back Now
The Class of 1998 has made history by becoming the first batch to pledge a record-breaking amount of Rs 8.25 Crores in commemoration of its Silver Jubilee Reunion at IIT Kanpur. It stands as a testament to their unwavering commitment to their alma mater, symbolizing a shared journey of growth and excellence. This endeavor goes beyond mere infrastructure improvement; it aims to cultivate a sense of community and belonging, enriching the experience for current and future members of the IITK family.
Class of 1998's unwavering commitment to leave behind a legacy of positive change, honoring the institution that shaped them will not only support institute's growth but also ensures that the spirit of giving back remains a cornerstone of the IIT Kanpur legacy.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback class of 1998 legacy project]

=== givingback class of 1999 legacy fund ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/class-of-1999-legacy-fund
Class of 1999 Legacy Fund
Give Back Now
Rs. 13,01,06,534
Pledges Received
Pledge Now
The Class of 1999 has initiated a campaign to contribute to IIT Kanpur's ongoing growth and its visionary plans for the future. Their objective is to raise funds to support the critical needs of the institute i.e. Infrastructure projects aimed at improving the living and learning experience of the students, strengthening the R&D ecosystem and lastly providing financial aid to the students from marginalised family backgrounds.
Wall of Pledges
Make a Pledge Give Back Now

=== givingback csr ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/csr
Explore CSR
Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) is the initiative taken by the corporate giants to integrate social and environmental concerns in their business practices. It is a way through which a company/corporate contributes towards economic, environmental and social development that brings positive impact on society at large. IIT Kanpur believes in Together We Grow, and thus invites corporates/companies to be an agent of such social change. Corporates/companies can now explore various possibilities of contributing towards the positive societal impact at the Institute.

=== givingback dr rajendra rathore fellowship fund ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/dr-rajendra-rathore-fellowship-fund
Dr Rajendra Rathore - A Tribute
Give Back Now
Dr. Rajendra (Raj) Rathore, Pfletschinger Habermann Professor of Organic Chemistry, departed this world on February 16, 2018, after a short but strenuous battle with pulmonary disease. Dr. Rathore was a simple person who made his way into the hearts of everyone who knew him. His greatest joy was his three girls: his wife, Rajni, and his two daughters, Richa and Risha. Raj was well known for the enormous energy that he put into his passions: taking care of his family, cooking elaborate meals and hosting large parties, and conducting cutting edge research.
Dr. Rathore was a pioneer in the field of organic and synthetic chemistry. He was born and grew up in Kannauj, and came to IIT Kanpur in 1982 to pursue his passion for science. As a student, Raj enjoyed spending his little free time in the lawns of Hall 5, playing badminton. He received his M.Sc. in Chemistry in 1986 from IIT Kanpur, under the mentorship of Prof. S. Chandrasekaran. Raj then traveled to the University of Western Ontario in London, Canada, and received his Ph.D. in Organic Chemistry in 1990. Raj did his postdoctoral research and visiting assistant professorship at the University of Houston under Jay K. Kochi, before joining the faculty at Marquette University in 2000.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback dr rajendra rathore fellowship fund]
Dr. Rathore made key contributions to the areas of organic supramolecular and materials chemistry. His interests focused in rational design and synthesis of novel electro-active molecules with applications in molecular recognition, photovoltaics, molecular electronics, and drug discovery. In particular, Raj is well known for his contributions to studies of the Scholl reaction mechanism, syntheses of cofacially arrayed polyfluorenes, and applications of nitric oxide complexes with aromatic donors. His recent work was focused on elucidating the application of frontier molecular orbitals (FMOs) in the rational design of novel charge-transfer materials.
Raj (and those who worked with him) would say that if you “drew him a structure, he could synthesize it.” Over the course of his distinguished career, he published more than 150 articles in various respected journals, including Science, Journal of the American Chemical Society, and Angewandte Chemie. He has an h-index of 35, and his papers have been cited more than 3,700 times; a testament to Raj’s impressive scientific career.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback dr rajendra rathore fellowship fund]
Raj Rathore is the rare product of the merging of immense talent and true passion. His contributions to science will be sorely missed, and the love and generosity he gave to his family and friends is a legacy that those who knew him will forever strive to live up to.
The goal of this fund
Donations made to support the Dr. Rajendra Rathore Seminar & Scholarship Fund will go towards funding an annual seminar, as well as the studies of a Masters student in the Chemistry department of IIT Kanpur. Raj's passion was for mentoring young scientists and helping those around him to achieve greatness; your donations help us to keep his enormous legacy alive.

=== givingback faculty recuritment fund ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/faculty-recuritment-fund.php
Faculty Recruitment Fund
17 Contributors
Give Back Now
To stay at the pinnacle of teaching and research, attracting young talent from across the globe is crucial. In the past this has been done by providing new faculty with grants/fellowships to boost their research and reward them for their achievementsIIT Kanpur has total faculty of 520 for 7,600 students, a ratio of 1:16. It is aiming to reach 600 faculty members next year i.e. 50 New Faculty Recruitment, to reach the ideal ratio of 1:10.To improve the student faculty ratio, the Institute has been making great efforts to recruit more faculties and has put in place several initiatives for it. The fund was of immense support to the Departments in running out processes in demand to promote faculty recruitments and arranging job talks for the aspiring faculty members across the globe.

=== givingback hall of residence ii ===

Hall of Residence II
2.6%
Rs. 5,39,785
Contributed	Rs. 2,10,00,000
Goal Amount
25 Contributors
Give Back Now
The Hall of Residence No. II for boys was constructed in year 1962-64. The Hall has seating capacity of 470 students with ground coverage of 6,895 m2 & the plinth area of Hall is 12,120 m2.
Existing condition of the Hall:
The Hall is 60 years old hence due to aging effect, there are cracks in the sunshade, parapet wall; common corridors etc.
There is seepage through the common corridor hence water proofing of whole common corridor is needed.
Need of Upgradation:
This Hall was externally & internally painted in 2013, hence as per norms both external & internal painting of the Hall is due.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback hall of residence ii]
This Hall has the cement concrete flooring in common corridors at ground floor of the building where as all other Halls have the Kota Stone Flooring.
This Hall being old does not have the provision of rain water harvesting where as Hall -X onwards all Halls have the provision of rain water harvesting.
Proposed Upgradation Works
The damaged sunshades are to be removed & same are to be re-casted.
The plaster of parapet wall of Hall is damaged hence same has to be removed & redone.
The complete external repairing & internal painting of Hall.
The terrace rain water harvesting of Hall.

Impact Stories: One Gift Can Change Everything
One Alumnus One Student Campaign
One of the most successful campaigns of IIT Kanpur, One Alumnus One Student, was launched to raise funds for 600 undergraduate students, who come from economically weaker sections of the society, to help provide them with IT hardware.

Campus Workers Campaign
IITK Students’ Gymkhana started a campaign to provide financial help to the workers of the Institute. Campus workers working in shops, eateries, canteens, and as vendors, rickshaw pullers etc. suffered heavy financial losses due the Covid-19 pandemic repeated lock downs.
Read More
Covid 19 Relief Operation
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback impact stories]
Convid 19 gave a sudden jolt to everyone. But the biggest shock was for the population without an assured income source. Out of estimated 36 lakh people in working age, only 1/7th have regular and salaried jobs. Rest are self employed, contractual labour and daily wagers.
Read More

=== givingback institute award ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/institute-award
Institute New Award
The award can be named after either the donor or any other person that the donor may decide. A separate ledger page is maintained in Dean R & A Office for each award donation.
A Utilization Report is sent to all donors at the end of the financial year by the Dean, R & A Office to inform about the financial status of endowment and the name of the recipient students/faculty/staff.
As per the Institute norms 4% of the interest earnings of the award endowment (approx. two third of interest earning) is utilized for award. The excess interest earned (after deducting the award amount) is added to the endowment to be used to cover drop in interest rates, inflation and growth in future.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback institute award]
Procedure for Instituting an Award:
The Academic Senate of the institute is the authorized body to approve the student award. Approval of Board of Governors (BOG) is required for Faculty award.
As soon as the donation and details for creating an award are received in the Institute, the proposal is sent to Senate through Chairman, Senate Scholarship and Prize Committee (SSPC) (in case of student award) or to BOG (in case of Faculty award) for approval of the award. The award is announced after getting approval from the Academic Senate/Board of Governors.
Enquiry Form For Instituting New Award
Gold Medal (to be given during Convocation) 22 Karat	15,00,000
Gold Medal (to be given during Convocation) 18 karat	12,00,000
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback institute award]
Silver Medal	2,00,000
In case the donor wants to institute a gold medal or silver medal of specific weight and purity, the endowment money required can be worked out accordingly based on the cost of the medal.
The weight of the Silver and Gold medal will be decided according to prevailing market rate

=== givingback kv smart classrooms ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/kv-smart-classrooms.php
 Smart Classrooms for a Smarter Tomorrow
  1 Contributors
0.1%
1 Contributors raised Rs. 5,000 of Goal Amount Rs. 70,00,000
Give Back Now
CAMPAIGN OBJECTIVE
Help us bring smart classrooms to KV IIT Kanpur
Kendriya Vidyalaya IIT Kanpur – the school right inside our campus needs 45 smart boards to transform how nearly 2,000 students learn every day.
45
Smart boards needed
₹1.5L
Cost per smart board
₹70L
Total goal amount
31 Dec 2026
Campaign Deadline
THE NEED
45 classrooms still run on chalk and blackboard
KV IITK has been part of this campus since 1965. Despite its legacy, the school has limited access to digital tools making it difficult for teachers to deliver engaging lessons and for students to keep pace with a rapidly changing world.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback kv smart classrooms]
Students lack access to visual, interactive content that makes complex topics easier to grasp
Differently-abled and slower learners have no assistive technology support
Private school peers already have digital classrooms, this gap keeps widening
Student enrollment has declined by 10% over the past four years, highlighting the need for more engaging and technology-enabled learning environments.
STUDENT VOICE
“The day our teacher showed us a video on the smart board, I finally understood what photosynthesis actually looks like. I just wish every class was like that.” - Class 7 student, KV IIT Kanpur
HOW IT WILL BENEFIT STUDENTS
One smart board. One classroom transformed.
More engaging learning: Lessons come alive videos, diagrams, and live demonstrations replace static text
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback kv smart classrooms]
Inclusive classrooms: Every student benefits.visual and assistive tools support all kinds of learners
Empowered teachers: Teachers save time on preparation and deliver better lessons
Future-ready skills: Students graduate with the digital fluency employers and universities expect
WHY NOW
The government is pushing for digital classrooms we can make it happen here
India’s National Education Policy (NEP) 2020 calls for technology-enabled, experiential learning in every classroom. KV IITK is ready to act, the only barrier is funding.
“I dream of a Digital India where quality education reaches the most inaccessible corners, driven by digital learning.”
- Shri Narendra Modi, Hon’ble Prime Minister of India
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback kv smart classrooms]
WHAT YOUR SUPPORT MEANS:
Wall of Donors recognition:Your name will be featured on the wall of donors.
Legacy impact: Smart boards have a long lifespan, benefiting thousands of KV students for years to come.
Tax benefit: your contribution qualifies for 100% tax benefits under section 80G in India and Section 501(c)(3) in the USA
A school on our campus. Children who deserve better.

=== givingback magik 2014 ===
Source: https://www.iitk.ac.in/dora/givingback/magik-2014
Small Gifts, Big Impact: The Power of 2014 MAGIK
Give Back Now
From those early morning classes to late-night study sessions, IIT Kanpur has been an integral part of our journey. You have shared laughter, triumphs, and challenges together with your friends. Now, it's time for you to give back to the institution that has given you so much. So, come together to ensure that future generations have the same opportunities to learn, grow, and succeed just as you did.
What is M.A.G.I.K.?
M.A.G.I.K. offers a straightforward way to donate and establish your batch corpus by making regular contributions through monthly instalments. This voluntary effort aims to create a legacy project fund specific to your graduating class, benefiting future students and enhancing the overall impact of IIT Kanpur. Funds collected by your batches will be spent based on the consensus of your batch.
[Context: IIT KANPUR DORA - GIVING BACK: FUNDS, SCHOLARSHIPS, CSR, CENTRES AND INITIATIVES > givingback magik 2014]
Why should you participate?
Your contribution can further support to your Alma Mater in the following way
Empowering deserving students by providing financial aid.
Elevating the campus experience by enhancing infrastructure.
Fueling groundbreaking inventions by supporting research.
Driving transformative social welfare initiatives.
Join hands to create MAGIK! For any queries reach out to dora_desk@iitk.ac.in
